| `f-svc-01` | framework | py:merged#118 php:affected rb:affected nd:merged#58 js:n/a dlp:n/a cli:n/a | py:ok php:? rb:? nd:ok js:n/a dlp:n/a cli:n/a | tina4-python **3.13.107** (released) 2026-08-20 | 2026-08-20 | In `ServiceRunner.stop()`, call `svc["instance"].stop()` for every service that carries one, before joining the threads. The reference is already stored for exactly this purpose and the storing line says so. | **`ServiceRunner.stop()` does not stop a class-based service, and leaks the thread.** **Proven end-to-end 2026-08-20 in `scratch/service-stop-leaks-thread/`** — a real `tina4 init python` app on released 3.13.107, re-runnable with `./prove.sh` (exits 1 on stock, 0 with the candidate patch). Stock: `stop()` took **5.0036 s**, the class service's counter went 510 at return to 529 200 ms later (delta 19), and `svc-class_service` was still in `threading.enumerate()`. With the patch: `stop()` **0.0050 s**, counter delta **0**, thread gone. The plain-callable control stopped promptly in both runs, confirming the `ctx.stop_event` path is untouched. The venv is left unpatched and the restore is md5-verified. `register_service()` stores `"instance": service` with the comment *keep reference so stop() can route to service.stop()* (`tina4_python/service/__init__.py:324`). `svc["instance"]` is written on that line and **read nowhere else in the file** — the routing the comment describes was never implemented. `ServiceRunner.stop()` sets `self._stop_event`, sets `svc["running"] = False`, and joins each thread with `timeout=5`; it never calls the instance's `stop()`. `Service.should_stop()` returns `not self._running`, and `self._running` is cleared only by `Service.stop()`. So the `while not self.should_stop():` loop the base class documents as the way to write a service never exits: `stop()` blocks the full 5.00 s join, gives up, and returns while the thread is still running. Measured on released 3.13.106: 6 loops at the moment `stop()` was called, **61 loops half a second after it returned**, and thread `svc-w` still alive in `threading.enumerate()`. Services registered as a plain callable via `register()` are unaffected — they observe `ctx.stop_event`, which `stop()` does set. This is the framework's own documented class-based pattern (`Service`'s docstring shows exactly this loop), and it is the path `PY-FW-02`'s proposed error message tells callers to move to, so `PY-FW-02` cannot be filed until this is fixed. Surfaced by the `PY-FW-02` fix review, which attributed it to an upstream backlog item `SVC-01` in `plan/v3/features/106-service-runner.md` — **that file does not exist and the string `SVC-01` appears nowhere in `origin/v3`** (checked 2026-08-20 against 3.13.107; `plan/` holds 27 tracked files, none of them a `v3/features/` tree). Treat the upstream attribution as unfounded and do not repeat it in a PR. The defect itself does not depend on it: reproduced here directly against the released package. **The documentation already promises this behaviour:** `docs/python/27-service-runner.md:224` states *"`stop()` calls `stop()` on each registered service and waits for their threads to finish"* — so the fix does not add a feature, it makes an existing published contract true. That is the justification the PR should lead with. **Fix written 2026-08-20:** local branch `fix/service-runner-stop-ignores-instance` @ `8cdc8d6` in `gitdir/tinaforks/tina4-python`, cut from `v3` at 3.13.107. `ServiceRunner.stop()` now calls `instance.stop()` inside a guarded `try`/`except` for every service carrying one, before the join loop; the plain-callable `register()` path is untouched. Two regression tests in `tests/test_service.py`, both proven to fail on unfixed `v3` and pass on the branch: the join-stall test fails `assert 5.000515460968018 < 2.0`, and the two-service isolation test (service A's `stop()` raises after `super().stop()`, service B must still stop) fails `assert 994 == 989`. Full suite on the branch: 5069 passed, 622 skipped, 5 failed — **all five reproduce identically on a clean `v3` worktree** (firebird connect timeout, three port-takeover contract tests, and the CLAUDE.md version check), so the 3.13.107 baseline is 5 pre-existing environment failures, not the 13 recorded on 3.13.105 in the `PY-FW-01`/`PY-FW-02` rows. **Filed 2026-08-20 as PR [#118](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/118)** against `v3` from fork `MichaelC8E/tina4-python`, branch `fix/service-runner-stop-ignores-instance` @ `8cdc8d6`, +126/-1. The PR leads with the documented contract at `27-service-runner.md:224` rather than framing it as a feature request. **Cross-port check 2026-08-24 — the same defect is live in php, ruby and nodejs, and was never ported.** The Python fix merged as [#118](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/118); no equivalent commit exists in any port. All three carry the identical setup and the identical omission: the instance is stashed at registration with a comment saying `stop()` will use it, and `stop()` never reads it. `tina4-php/Tina4/ServiceRunner.php:80-89` stashes `'instance' => $service` under the comment *Stash the instance so stop() can call $service->stop()*, and `stop()` (`:208-235`) sends SIGTERM to a PID, writes a stop file, and clears status — the word `instance` appears three times in the whole file, all three at the registration site, never in `stop()`. `tina4-ruby/lib/tina4/service_runner.rb:80` stores `instance: service` and `stop()` (`:130-154`) sets `ctx.running = false` and joins with a 5s timeout; `instance` appears twice in the file, once in an ArgumentError string and once at the store. `tina4-nodejs/packages/core/src/service.ts:269-273` stashes it under the comment *so future stop() calls can route to service.stop()* — the word *future* is an accurate description of code that was never written — and `stop()` (`:359-372`) sets `context.running = false` and clears the interval. In each port the stored reference is **written once and read nowhere**, which is exactly the test that characterised the Python defect. Checked by source read at 3.13.114, not executed; the Python row's own reproduction should be re-run per port before a fix is filed. `tina4-js` has no service runner (`src/` is api, core, debug, i18n, pwa, router, rtc, sse, storage, ws) and `tina4delphi` contains no `ServiceRunner` symbol at all, hence `n/a` for both. **Record:** was `PY-FW-16`; filing — [#118](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/118) — merged into `v3` 2026-08-21 as `7a3608b9a`; `ServiceRunner.stop()` now routes to the instance at `tina4_python/service/__init__.py:359`. **nodejs ported and merged 2026-08-24.** The source-read claim above was confirmed by execution: proven end-to-end in `scratch/nodejs-service-stop-does-not-stop-the-service/` against released `tina4-nodejs@3.13.114` from npm, re-runnable with `./prove.sh` (exit 1 on stock, 0 with the candidate patch, and the installed bundle is restored md5-verified). Stock: the class-based service ran **57 more loops after `stop()` returned**, `run()` never exited, and `isRunning("class_service")` returned `false` while the loop was still counting — so the runner reports a service stopped that is still running. The plain-callable control registered via `register()` stopped with delta **0**, confirming only the class path is broken. Node has no threads, so unlike Python there is no 5 s join to stall: `stop()` returns in 0.24 ms and the loop simply survives it. Second mechanism the Python row does not have: `executeHandler` passes the context to the handler (`packages/core/src/service.ts:114`) but `Tina4Service.asHandler()` discards its argument (`:215-219`), so a class-based service cannot observe `context.running` either — `Tina4Service.stop()` was the only stop path, and nothing called it. **Fix:** branch `fix/service-runner-stop-ignores-instance` @ `83ac514`, cut from `v3` at `2572572`. Adds `instance?: Tina4Service` to `RegisteredService`, which lets `registerService` drop its `as unknown as Record<string, unknown>` cast, and `stop()` now calls `instance.stop()` in a `try`/`catch` that reports through `Log.error` rather than swallowing. Three new assertions in `test/service.test.ts` proven red on stock and green on the fix — reverting only `service.ts` to `origin/v3` gives `53 passed, 3 failed` (`counter frozen after stop expected 10, got 25`; `run() exited after stop`; `sibling counter frozen expected 10, got 19`), and the branch gives `56 passed, 0 failed`. Full suite measured both ways on the same tree: stock `origin/v3` **7639 passed, 33 failed, 239 skipped across 335 files**; the branch **7646 passed, 33 failed, 239 skipped** — the same 16 failing files in both (mongo, memcached, redis, greenmail and docker environment gates), so the +7 are exactly the new assertions and the fix adds no regression. `npm run typecheck` exits 0. **Filed as PR [#58](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-nodejs/pull/58)** (+138/-3) and **merged into `v3` 2026-08-24 as `ead390c`, with zero reviews**; upstream `packages/core/src/service.ts:369` now carries the call, and it shipped in **3.13.116** (`ab0a9fb`). php and ruby remain `affected` and unported. | `PYTHONPATH=<clone at origin/v3> python3` — subclass `Service` with `run()` looping on `while not self.should_stop()` and a counter, `register_service()`, `start()`, sleep, then time `stop()`: it takes 5.00 s and the counter keeps climbing afterwards while the `svc-<name>` thread stays in `threading.enumerate()` |
| `f-cache-01` | framework | py:merged#117 php:merged#198 rb:merged#40 nd:merged#52 js:n/a dlp:n/a cli:n/a | py:? php:? rb:? nd:? js:n/a dlp:n/a cli:n/a | tina4-python **3.13.107** (released) 2026-08-20 | 2026-08-20 | Extend `ResponseCache._may_store`: refuse storage when the response carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, `private` or `no-cache` (RFC 9111 s3), and treat a request `Cookie` header the way `Authorization` is already treated — store only if the response explicitly allows shared caching. Separately, fold the `Vary` values into the cache key so `Vary: Cookie` produces a correct per-user hit instead of a permanent miss. Ship this before `PY-FW-01`. | **Cross-user response replay in `ResponseCache`, live in shipped code and independent of `PY-FW-01`.** **Proven over real HTTP 2026-08-20 in `scratch/response-cache-replays-across-sessions/`** — a real `tina4 init python` app on released 3.13.107 driven through the genuine front controller, not the in-process `TestClient` (which the framework's own `plan/testclient-front-controller-d6.md` says re-implements dispatch and "fabricates responses the server never sends"). `./prove.sh` exits 2 on stock, 0 patched. Stock: ALICE got `X-Cache: MISS` with `{"user":"Alice Smith","account_number":"ACC-9921","balance":"$84,250.00"}`; BOB, sending his own `Cookie: session=bob_session_token` to the same URL, got `X-Cache: HIT` and **ALICE's body** — the handler never ran. `Cache-Control: no-store` was ignored identically. Patched: BOB receives his own record, `no-store` is honoured, and both controls still return `X-Cache: HIT`, proving the fix does not simply disable caching. Venv left unpatched, restore md5-verified. **Filed 2026-08-20 as PR [#117](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/117)** against `v3` from fork `MichaelC8E/tina4-python`, branch `fix/response-cache-leaks-across-sessions` @ `2a2d8f5`, +143/-0. `ResponseCache` keys entries on method plus URL only — `cache_key()` returns `f"GET:{url}"` and the class docstring states *"Cache key: method + URL (including query string)"* — and `_may_store` (`cache/__init__.py`) guards exactly two things: `Vary: *`, and the `Authorization` request header. A request carrying `Cookie` and no `Authorization` falls through to a bare `return True`. Since Tina4's own session mechanism is a cookie, every session-authenticated response is storable and replayable. **There is no opt-out:** the strings `no-store`, `private` and `no-cache` appear nowhere in `tina4_python/cache/__init__.py` (the only match is an unrelated comment at line 1249), so a handler cannot refuse caching even with the correct standard header. **This does not need `@cached` to be fixed first — it is reachable on released 3.13.107 through the documented middleware path.** `docs/python/11-caching.md:178` teaches `@get("/api/products", middleware=["ResponseCache:300"])` verbatim; the string form resolves through the registry at `core/router.py:703-707` and the class is exported at `tina4_python/__init__.py:137`. **Measured on stock 3.13.107, no patch applied:** a first request with `Cookie: session=ALICE` stored `{"user":"ALICE","cart_total":"R 1499.00"}`; a second request to the same URL with `Cookie: session=BOB` was served ALICE's body, both keying to `GET:/api/me`. Repeated with `Cache-Control: no-store`, `private` and `no-cache` on the response — all three were stored and replayed to the other session. **The safety machinery already exists but is unreachable:** `_vary_matches` implements RFC 9111 s4.1 correctly and compares stored `Vary` values against the incoming request, so a `Vary: Cookie` response is never mis-served — but because the key ignores `Vary`, ALICE's and BOB's entries collide on one slot and each request evicts the other, turning correctness into a permanent miss rather than a correct per-user hit. **Relationship to `PY-FW-01`:** that row is about `@cached` being inert. Fixing it without fixing this one widens the same leak to every `@cached` route, including the framework's own `example/` app. This row must land first. Not documented on any page — the backlog is where framework faults live. **Cross-port check 2026-08-24 — fixed in every port, ported by the maintainer on the same day.** Within hours of [#117](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/117) merging, the same fix landed in all three ports under the same commit title, *fix(cache): stop ResponseCache replaying one session's response to another*: tina4-php `bb882889` (2026-08-21, upstream php PR 198), tina4-ruby `6f0a8b2` (2026-08-21, ruby PR 40), tina4-nodejs `3000705` (2026-08-21, nodejs PR 52). Verified by reading the shipped guard in each rather than trusting the commit title: `tina4-php/Tina4/Middleware/ResponseCache.php:772` refuses when `array_intersect($this->cacheControlTokens($response), ['no-store', 'private', 'no-cache'])` is non-empty; `tina4-ruby/lib/tina4/response_cache.rb:510` refuses unless the intersection of the response tokens with `no-store private no-cache` is empty; `tina4-nodejs/packages/core/src/cache.ts:1638` defines `NO_STORE_DIRECTIVES` and `:1717` returns false on any of them. Python's own merged form is at `tina4_python/cache/__init__.py:1408` on `origin/v3` — `if directives & {"no-store", "private", "no-cache"}`. All four also keep the pre-existing Authorization guard and extend the reasoning to session cookies. **This is the first recorded case of a fix of ours being ported cross-language by the maintainer without being asked**, which is worth knowing before filing a port-specific issue that may already be closed. `tina4-js` has no server-side response cache and `tina4delphi` contains no `ResponseCache` symbol, hence `n/a`. **Record:** was `PY-FW-17`; filing — [#117](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/117) — merged into `v3` 2026-08-21 as `350777ac5`; `ResponseCache._may_store` now refuses `no-store`, `private` and `no-cache` responses. | `PYTHONPATH=<clone at v3> python3` — instantiate `ResponseCache(ttl=300)`, call `before_cache`/`after_cache` with a request stub carrying `method="GET"`, `url="/api/me"`, `headers={"Cookie": "session=ALICE"}` and a response stub whose `_headers` hold the pairs; store a body naming ALICE, then call `before_cache` again with `Cookie: session=BOB` — the returned response object carries ALICE's body. Repeat with `("Cache-Control", "no-store")` on the stored response for the no-opt-out half. |
| `f-orm-01` | framework | py:merged#119 php:clear rb:merged#39 nd:clear js:n/a dlp:n/a cli:n/a | py:? php:? rb:? nd:? js:n/a dlp:n/a cli:n/a | tina4-python **3.13.108** / `v3` 2026-08-21 — `adapter.py`, `postgres.py` and `tests/test_database_connect_timeout.py` verified byte-identical to upstream `v3` on the day of measurement, so these numbers are not from a patched tree. Reproduced deterministically 2026-08-21. Seen as intermittent CI failures in upstream runs [32222423418](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/actions/runs/32222423418) on `v3` and [32404411501](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/actions/runs/32404411501) on [#117](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/117) | 2026-08-20 | **Measure the gate on the driver's clock as well as our own.** `connect_deadline` (`tina4_python/database/adapter.py:114-127`) decides whether a driver failure was the bound firing by comparing a `time.monotonic()` reading against `seconds` — but libpq measures its own `connect_timeout` on `CLOCK_REALTIME`. Stamp both (`started = time.monotonic()` and `started_real = time.time()`) and translate when `max(time.monotonic() - started, time.time() - started_real) >= seconds`. Keeping the monotonic reading inside the `max` is what stops a BACKWARD realtime step from hiding a genuine timeout; adding the realtime reading is what stops a FORWARD one from leaking the driver's message. It costs two clock reads and changes no timing — the driver still aborts at its own deadline. **Measured 2026-08-21** against the reproduction in the last column: unfixed leaks at both 1.3x and 2.0x realtime-vs-monotonic divergence; this closes both, at `elapsed=1.5629s` and `1.5146s`, i.e. without lengthening the wait. *Rejected, and it is what this row previously recommended:* `driver_connect_timeout_seconds` returning `max(1, floor(seconds) + 1)` instead of `max(1, ceil(seconds))`. It buys exactly one second of tolerance, so it closes 1.3x (`elapsed=2.5641s`) but still LEAKS at 2.0x, and it inflates the operator's configured N into N+1 on the failure path (a 2s bound waits 3s; the shipped 10s default waits 11s) — the very thing `connect_deadline`'s docstring rejects when it refuses "giving the driver a GRACE so an outer timer expires first". Applying both also works, if maintainers want slack as well as a correct comparison. Either way `adapter.py:109-112` has to be rewritten: it states the invariant as the driver's OPTION versus our bound, when what decides the race is the driver's ABORT INSTANT versus our monotonic reading. Existing pinned assertions survive all three variants — `test_driver_option_is_never_shorter_than_the_configured_bound` asserts only `option >= configured`, and `test_fractional_bound_is_rounded_up_for_whole_second_driver_options` pins `2.5` to `3` and `0.2` to `1`, which `floor(s)+1` also satisfies. A regression test needs no clock faking: strengthening that first assertion from `>=` to `>` fails on unfixed source for every whole bound. **Proven 2026-08-21** in `scratch/connect-timeout-loses-to-the-drivers-clock/` against released tina4-python **3.13.107** from PyPI (psycopg2-binary 2.9.12, bundled libpq 170009), whose `adapter.py` and `postgres.py` were verified byte-identical to upstream `v3`. `./prove.sh` runs the stock probe and `./prove.sh --fixed` takes a variant argument of `a`, `b` or `ab`; the venv is restored on an EXIT trap and the restore verified against the stock md5 `695ed6b2b98c8f66ab1ff502af9e9024`. Measured there, bound 2s: stock holds at 1.0x on both a silent and a dribbling server (2.0162s, 2.0151s) and BREAKS at 1.3x (1.5641s) and 2.0x (1.5157s); A holds all four and still fails in 2.0s; B holds 1.3x (2.5652s) but BREAKS at 2.0x (1.5165s) and stretches every failure to 3.0s; A+B holds all four. So **A is what closes the defect, and B closes nothing A does not** — B earns its place only by removing the dependence on the gate for a driver that fires early for reasons other than clock divergence, which matters because mysql-connector, pymssql and pyodbc each run their own timer and only libpq's was measured. Both were applied on the fork branch `fix/connect-timeout-message-loses-to-driver-clock` (cut from `v3`), with `bound_was_reached` extracted as a pure function so the decision is testable without faking a clock. Tests added: the strict-option assertion plus five cases on `bound_was_reached`. Verified failing on unfixed source two ways — with the fix stashed the test file cannot even collect, and with the helper present but `ceil` restored the strict test fails on `assert 1 > 1.0`. `tests/test_database_connect_timeout.py` on the branch: 22 passed, 4 skipped, 1 pre-existing environment failure (`libfbclient.so` absent locally). | **The framework's connect-timeout message loses a race its own docstring says it cannot lose, because the framework and the driver measure the bound on two different clocks.** `connect_deadline` (`tina4_python/database/adapter.py:115`) stamps `started = time.monotonic()` (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC`) and translates a driver failure into `DatabaseConnectTimeout` only when `elapsed >= seconds` (`:119`). libpq measures its own `connect_timeout` with `gettimeofday()` — `CLOCK_REALTIME`. Verified rather than assumed: the bundled driver binary `.venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/psycopg2/_psycopg.cpython-313-x86_64-linux-gnu.so` imports `gettimeofday@GLIBC_2.2.5` and imports no `clock_gettime` at all. The two clocks are independent — NTP slews and steps `CLOCK_REALTIME` and never touches `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` — so whenever realtime has run fast relative to monotonic during the connect, libpq reaches its deadline before the framework's monotonic `elapsed` reaches the bound, the gate declines to translate, and the caller gets the bare driver text instead of a message naming `TINA4_DATABASE_CONNECT_TIMEOUT`. **And there is no margin to absorb it:** `driver_connect_timeout_seconds` (`:64-74`) rounds the driver's option UP with `ceil`, so for any whole bound `ceil(N) == N` and the two deadlines are nominally the same instant. `DEFAULT_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0` (`:26`) and the documented `TINA4_DATABASE_CONNECT_TIMEOUT=10` (`tina4_python/CLAUDE.md:1645`) are both whole, so the default deployment is the affected case, not an edge case. The only thing normally holding `elapsed` above the bound is that `started` is stamped a few hundred microseconds before libpq starts its own clock. **Reproduced deterministically 2026-08-21** through the real `PostgreSQLAdapter.connect` with a 2s bound: with realtime slewed 1.3x, libpq aborted at monotonic `1.5629s` and `psycopg2.OperationalError: connection to server at "127.0.0.1", port N failed: timeout expired` reached the caller — the exact CI text. Control at 1.0x: `DatabaseConnectTimeout` at `2.0143s`. **Three hypotheses were tested and are wrong, and this row previously recorded the first of them as the cause.** (a) *Incidental overhead or CPU contention flipping a roughly 1ms margin* — structurally impossible: every microsecond spent before or after libpq's own wait only ADDS to `elapsed`, so no amount of overhead or load can pull it below the bound (measured: under 112 busy-loops the margin GREW to `+0.019544s`). That is what narrows the cause to a clock disagreement rather than a timing accident. (b) *A different bundled libpq on CI* — the CI log shows `+ psycopg2-binary==2.9.11`, the same statically bundled `__libpq_version__ == 170006` as local. (c) *libpq quantising its deadline to whole seconds* (the pre-17 `time_t` arithmetic) — refuted on libpq 170006: 30 connects started within 23 microseconds of a wall-clock second boundary all took `2.002s`, and forcing a late second poll round also took `2.001s`. **Affects only the two adapters that lean on the driver's own timer** — `postgres.py:277` and `mysql.py:61`. The watchdog adapters (`mssql`, `odbc`, `firebird`) time on a worker thread via `call_with_deadline`, monotonic on both sides, and are not exposed to this comparison. **Consequence beyond CI noise:** an operator who set the variable and then hits a wedged server intermittently gets a message that does not name the variable they set, which is the entire reason the feature exists. Not documented on any page — framework faults go to the backlog. **Exposure measured 2026-08-21:** logging every `gettimeofday` call during a silent black-hole connect (Python reads `clock_gettime`, so `gettimeofday` calls are libpq's alone) shows five reads — four clustered inside the first 55 microseconds, then one when `poll()` returns at 2000.99 ms. libpq therefore computes its deadline and its final remaining budget within a 55-microsecond window, and only a forward realtime jump landing inside THAT window shortens the wait. The same trace shows libpq rounding its remaining budget UP to whole milliseconds — it waited 2000.94 ms after a read at 0.055 ms — so libpq never fires early of its own accord, which rules out millisecond truncation on a re-poll. That bounds per-connect exposure to roughly 55 microseconds: rare, consistent with an intermittent CI failure, but not on its own a full account of the observed rate. No other mechanism survives, because work before or after libpq's wait can only ADD to `elapsed`. **Cross-port check 2026-08-24 — one port needed the same fix, two are immune by design, and the reasons differ.** **ruby: fixed, ported the same day.** `tina4-ruby` `a7dfba4` (2026-08-21, ruby PR 39) carries the same commit title as our `30c81d2`, *fix(database): compare the connect bound on the driver's own clock*. `lib/tina4/database_adapter.rb:314-317` is `bound_reached?(elapsed_monotonic, elapsed_realtime, seconds)` returning `[elapsed_monotonic, elapsed_realtime].max >= seconds`, and `:284-286` is `connect_timeout_whole_seconds` returning `[seconds.floor + 1, 1].max` — both halves of the Python fix, same shape. Ruby is exposed for the same reason Python is: the `pg` gem is libpq, which times `connect_timeout` on `gettimeofday`. **php: clear, and immune by a different and arguably better mechanism.** `tina4-php/Tina4/Database/ConnectTimeoutTrait.php` samples `microtime(true)` — wall clock, the *same* domain libpq uses, so there is no cross-domain race to lose — and `:123-130` additionally short-circuits on the driver's own diagnosis: it returns early only when `$elapsed < $armed && !$driverReportedTimeout`, so a driver saying *timeout expired* produces the framework's contract message no matter what PHP's own clock reads. Its in-source comment documents the exact symptom this row chased, including *"GitHub Actions observed ext-pgsql returning 'timeout expired' just below the PHP-side comparison"*, and records measured overshoots at a 3s bound (ext-pgsql 3.002781s, pdo_pgsql 3.003829s, mysqli 3.002987s, ext-mongodb 3.003139s). **nodejs: clear, immune by driver architecture.** `packages/orm/src/connectTimeout.ts:226` times on `performance.now()`, which is monotonic, and all four of Node's clients — pg, mysql2, tedious, and the Mongo selection loop — arm plain JS `setTimeout`, so no realtime clock is involved anywhere in the comparison and the two-domain mismatch cannot arise. Its `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_TOLERANCE_MS = 50` (`:123`) solves an unrelated problem, libuv's coarse loop time letting a timer fire before `performance.now()` agrees, measured over 60 rounds at a 200ms budget (Linux x64 Node v24.18.0 earliest -0.7125ms, darwin arm64 Node v24.9.0 earliest +0.0872ms). All three ports checked by source read at 3.13.114, not by executing the skew harness. `tina4-js` has no database layer and `tina4delphi`'s only `ConnectTimeout` is an HTTP client option (`Tina4Core.pas:1064`), not a database connect bound, hence `n/a` for both. **Record:** was `PY-FW-18`; filing — [#119](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/119) — opened 2026-08-21 against `v3` from fork `MichaelC8E/tina4-python`, branch `fix/connect-timeout-message-loses-to-driver-clock` @ `30c81d2`, rebased onto `0621777` (3.13.108), +133/-20; merged into `v3` 2026-08-21 as `e34d2a400`. | Deterministic, no mocks, about 5 seconds. (1) Confirm the clock domains: run `nm -D --undefined-only` over `.venv/lib/python3.13/site-packages/psycopg2/_psycopg*.so` and filter for `gettimeofday` and `clock_gettime` — it prints `gettimeofday@GLIBC_2.2.5` and nothing else. (2) Build an `LD_PRELOAD` shim that runs `CLOCK_REALTIME` fast and leaves `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` alone: intercept `gettimeofday`, `clock_gettime` (skewing only `CLOCK_REALTIME`) and `time`, scaling the offset from process start by a `SKEW_RATE` environment variable. (3) Start a listening socket that reads libpq's 8-byte SSLRequest, replies `b"N"`, reads the StartupMessage, waits 1.5s, sends the single byte `b"R"` (1 of the 5 bytes of an auth-request header) and then never writes again — that byte is what forces libpq to poll a second time and recompute its remaining budget from realtime. (4) Run `TINA4_DATABASE_CONNECT_TIMEOUT=2 SKEW_RATE=1.3 LD_PRELOAD=./skew.so` against `PostgreSQLAdapter().connect("postgresql://u:p@127.0.0.1:PORT/d")`. Expect `psycopg2.OperationalError ... timeout expired` at about 1.56s instead of `DatabaseConnectTimeout`; with `SKEW_RATE=1.0` the same script raises `DatabaseConnectTimeout` at about 2.01s. A silent black hole that never writes at all cannot show this: with a single poll round libpq converts its deadline to a relative `poll()` timeout immediately, and the kernel measures that on monotonic. |

The ports release on independent cadences, so their version numbers are not all present as tags in tina4-python. **Measured 2026-08-24 by comparing the full tag sets:** tina4-php has 5 released `3.13.x` tags with no tina4-python counterpart (`3.13.13`, `3.13.17`, `3.13.18`, `3.13.80`, `3.13.93`), tina4-ruby has 3 (`3.13.17`, `3.13.18`, `3.13.93`) and tina4-nodejs has 3 (`3.13.18`, `3.13.20`, `3.13.25`). **Confirmed live against the real URL the installer builds:** `HTTP 404` for tags `3.13.93`, `3.13.18` and `3.13.80`; `HTTP 200` for `3.13.114`. A 404 is a clean HTTP response, so the retry logic correctly declines to retry it — and the caller then skips the skill with no message, no warning, and a zero exit status. The failure mode is already recorded in their own source from a different cause: `tina4-ruby/lib/tina4/ai.rb:77` notes that a single dropped fetch left `installed` missing `tina4-maintainer` *with no other symptom*. A user on an affected version runs `tina4 ai`, sees success, and ends up with the language developer skill but neither shared skill. **Currently dormant, not fixed:** `3.13.103`–`3.13.114` are contiguous across all four repos and all four sit on `3.13.114` today, so it does not fire on current versions. It refires on the next version skew, which the independent release cadences make a matter of when, not if. **python is immune by construction** — it is the repo being fetched from, so its ref always resolves. **Record:** was `ALL-FW-02`; scope — php, ruby, nodejs (python is the source repo and is immune by construction). | Pick a port version with no matching tina4-python tag and request the URL the installer builds: `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/3.13.93/.claude/skills/tina4-maintainer/SKILL.md` returns `404`; substituting `3.13.114` returns `200` |

Evidence: `scratch/skill-drift-gate-skips-siblings-and-two-trees/`. **FILED 2026-08-25.** tina4-python [#124](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/124), tina4-php [#205](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-php/pull/205), tina4-ruby [#44](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-ruby/pull/44), tina4-nodejs [#60](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-nodejs/pull/60) — one PR per language, each rebased onto that repo's 3.13.117 release and re-verified after the rebase. | In any of the four port repos, read `.cursor/skills/tina4-maintainer/SKILL.md` as bytes: the first three bytes are the UTF-8 BOM, and decoding as UTF-8 shows the mojibake sequence in place of each em dash in the description |

Stock (md5 `93e24f04394dec1f2dc1f72ca653e6d7`) reproduces 3 of 3 defects; the replacement gates all 3. **The fix:** absent siblings are printed as `ABSENT ... NOT compared` and count toward a non-zero exit, with `--siblings-optional` for a single-repo checkout that says in its own output that the result is partial; all three trees are compared, tree against the same tree, because `.agents`/`.cursor` legitimately differ from `.claude` for tina4-maintainer (755-byte entrypoints, not copies) and a gate that compares those against `.claude` is wrong; `tina4-developer-<lang>` is gated inside its own repo; and an encoding assertion runs in both modes and never auto-repairs. **Fixture C is a negative control** — three new assertions are exactly the kind that produce false positives, and a gate that fails on a clean tree gets switched off. **One deliberate exclusion:** the global install is compared with `-x evals -x '*.skill'`, because `tina4 ai` fetches `SKILL.md` and `references/` and never the eval fixtures or the packaged bundle; without it the gate is permanently red, which is the same failure mode by another route. Scoped to the global target only. **First real run found two things this ledger did not have** — see `f-ai-04` for the live tina4-js drift and `f-ai-07` for the missing reference material. **FILED 2026-08-25.** tina4-python [#124](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-python/pull/124), tina4-php [#205](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-php/pull/205), tina4-ruby [#44](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-ruby/pull/44), tina4-nodejs [#60](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-nodejs/pull/60) — one PR per language, each rebased onto that repo's 3.13.117 release and re-verified after the rebase. | Run `bash scripts/sync-tina4-skills.sh --check` from a checkout with no sibling repos beside it — it reports on the global install only, and never mentions that php, ruby and nodejs were not examined |
| `f-ai-06` | framework | py:affected php:affected rb:affected nd:affected js:clear dlp:clear cli:n/a | py:? php:? rb:? nd:? js:? dlp:? cli:n/a | skill text at tags **3.13.103** and **3.13.114** · codex-cli **0.147.0** 2026-08-24 | 2026-08-24 | **Patch written 2026-08-25 and verified to apply cleanly to all twelve files (4 skills x 3 trees) — `scratch/dev-skill-delegates-to-a-worker-the-agent-does-not-have/suggested-fix.md`, runnable as `apply-fix.sh`; NOT applied, not branched, not filed.** Six edits, same in all four copies, **reordered 2026-08-24 by what the evidence supports**. **(1)** Finish `de5358d`: remove the five approval / ask-first gates it was meant to drop and did not — `:236` approval-to-start, `:850` confirm-to-close, and the three UI questions at `:409`, `:483`, `:861`. **(2)** Reframe the announcement contract (`:29-61`) so a line precedes an action *in the same turn* rather than being described as the developer's chance to stop it; the stop-point framing is what turns a progress line into an end-of-turn. The ablation shows this block is separable and costs nothing to change. **(3)** Make the delegation conditional on the harness actually having workers, and keep the plan file unconditional — name the check instead of assuming the answer. **(4)** Delete the contradiction: either the preamble at `:122` or the imperative at `:156` has to go, not both. **(5)** Correct `:161`, which names Codex and Cursor as if a model/effort selector were a worker. **(6)** Reconcile the two plan doctrines — the Working Method at `:120-257` and *Plan First — Always* at `:764-868` restate the same rules in different words, and `:769`'s *"No exceptions"* is the stricter of the two. | **The developer skill tells the assistant not to do the work itself and to spawn a worker — a harness capability the agent it is shipped to does not have.** `.claude/skills/tina4-developer-nodejs/SKILL.md:155`: *"When the developer gives an instruction, don't do the work inline. **Allocate it to a plan, then spawn a separate worker to execute it**, so the main session is always free for the next input."* Identical text at `tina4-developer-python/SKILL.md:152`, `-php/SKILL.md:157`, `-ruby/SKILL.md:155` — the four files are the same document with the language swapped. **The capability is not there.** `codex debug prompt-input` renders the whole model-visible prompt for a tina4 project: the word *worker* appears **zero** times in it; the skill uses it **17** times. Nothing in the section says what to do when no worker mechanism exists, so the agent is left holding an instruction it cannot execute and the surrounding ceremony — plan file, announcement, report — is all it can still perform. **Three compounding defects.** (1) The instruction is unconditional and has no fallback; the one sentence about cross-agent portability, `:161` *"This is agent-agnostic: Claude maps it to model + reasoning-effort, Codex to its model/effort selector, Cursor to its model picker"*, is about **cost tiers**, not about whether workers exist — it reads as a portability guarantee while assuming the capability it is meant to make portable. (2) It contradicts its own preamble 33 lines earlier, `:122` *"Prefer keeping the main session free … **but if you build in the main session**, you still own the plan file"* — the preamble permits inline work, §1's heading and first sentence forbid it. (3) `Announce before you act` (`:29-46`, added 2026-08-19 in `5cf5c5b`, first shipped at **3.13.105**) asks for three announcements per action and frames their purpose as interception: *"A developer who can see the plan can stop it before you spend their afternoon undoing it"*, *"one line before each step, **so the developer can stop between steps** rather than after all of them. Formula: `About to: <verb> <path or command>`"*. Told the point of an announcement is to let the developer intervene, and told separately that the session must stay free "for the next input", a turn-taking agent has every reason to end the turn on the announcement. **Reporter-raised**, from a Codex session on a tina4-nodejs app where the assistant repeatedly announced work and did little or none of it. **The report carries three literal fingerprints of this file and of nothing else in the Tina4 tree:** every reply prefixed 🤖 (`SKILL.md:27` *"begin every reply with the 🤖 emoji"*), the `About to: <verb> <path>` formula three times (`:40`), and a maintained `plan/release-readiness.md` (`:122` *"Work is driven by a plan file under `plan/`"*). The 🤖 marker and the worker instruction are both present at tag **3.13.103**, the version that app pins; `Announce before you act` is **not**, first appearing at **3.13.105** — so the skill in that developer's context was ≥3.13.105 even though the app pins 3.13.103, which `skillsRef()` keys the fetch to. **Measured, isolated three-arm run** (proof project [`scratch/dev-skill-delegates-to-a-worker-the-agent-does-not-have`](../scratch/dev-skill-delegates-to-a-worker-the-agent-does-not-have/)): same fixture, same prompt, `HOME` redirected so the machine's own global skills cannot leak — arm C (project `.agents/skills/`) 4× `About to:` / 5× 🤖, arm E (`AGENTS.md` pointer → `.claude/skills/`, what `tina4 ai` writes) 8× / 7× and the only arm to build a `plan/` tree, arm D (no skill, control) **0× / 0×**. The contract is the skill's, not Codex's. **NOT proven: a stall.** All three arms finished the task — `codex exec` is non-interactive, so "the next input" and "stop between steps" have no referent and the cost lands as overhead instead (arm E 10 423 log lines against the control's 6 534). The reported failure is multi-turn and interactive; reproducing it needs a driven interactive session. Do not upgrade this row past that without one. **`js:clear` is a finding, not an omission** — `tina4-js/SKILL.md:84-107` carries a *softened* copy of the same Working Method (*"Prefer a worker per task; main session stays free **when possible**"*, and the Delegate row keeps its "(or you)" escape) and never says "don't do the work inline". **Correction 2026-08-24:** an earlier draft of this row said tina4-js also gates on developer approval, citing *"the plan file (approved to start)"* and *"scope it with the developer first"*. Those phrases are in the **tina4-js repo's own copy** (v1.5.0), which is not what ships — the copy `tina4 ai` serves is the port copy (v1.5.2, md5 `ef7829e56b7c`), and there the gates are already gone: the Plan row reads *"the plan file (outcome stated, then start)"* and the rescope sentence reads *"scope it and state the outcome, then create `plan/<feature>.md` and start"*. The shipped tina4-js skill has no approval gate at all. `sync-tina4-skills.sh` calls the tina4-js repo's copy *"a soft mirror on its own release cadence"* and does not gate it, so the stale gated text reaches contributors reading that repo, not end users. **`dlp:clear`** — tina4delphi ships `skills/rad-studio-delphi` and `skills/rad-studio-maintainer`, neither of which contains the instruction or the `About to:` formula. **Record:** was `ALL-FW-06`; scope — all four developer skills (python, php, ruby, nodejs). **Deep pass 2026-08-24 — the gate inventory, the history, an ablation, and one reversal.** **(a) The file carries twelve turn-ending or action-suppressing instructions against four that push the other way, and it names its own failure mode in passing.** Gates: `:44` never more than two files between announcements; `:48` five named stop-points (first file write, migration, dependency, scaffolding past two files, full test suite); `:131` the Delegate row; `:156` don't do the work inline; `:236` *"Developer approval is only required to **start** the plan"*; `:409`, `:483` and `:861` three separate ask-first UI gates (*"Before writing any UI code, ask: 'Are we server-rendered or client-rendered?'"*); `:769` every feature needs a plan file, *"No exceptions"*; `:850` `## Status: Complete` only *"After developer confirmation"*; `:939` *"Ask short questions"*. Counter-gates: `:137-152` infer the outcome and PROCEED, *"Do not stop to ask"*; `:233-238` *"do not wait for per-item approval … that is why plans stall"*; `:835` the same; `:122` *"but if you build in the main session"*. **(b) The maintainers diagnosed this five months in and the fix missed its own target.** `de5358d` (2026-08-13) is titled *"docs(skills): let AI complete plans autonomously (drop approval-to-start / confirm-to-close / ask-first gates)"*. It rewrote the outcome-asking section and the two Working Method table rows, and it **never touched any of the five gates above**: `git show de5358d -- .claude/skills/tina4-developer-nodejs/SKILL.md` matched none of them, and `git blame` puts `:236`, `:850` and `:861` at `16eb0c8` (2026-08-11) and `:409`, `:483` at `6d7c3568` (2026-07-08) — both **before** the commit that claimed to remove them. All five are still at HEAD, in `.claude` and `.agents` alike. **(c) History explains the shape.** The worker operating model arrived `0d84ef9` 2026-07-09, when `.claude/skills` was the only tree in the repo. `.agents/skills` — the tree Codex reads — was created four weeks later, `33c0a0b` 2026-08-04 (*"docs: add Codex skill entrypoints"*), by copying the operating model across without checking that Codex can execute it; `.cursor/skills` followed at `eece8fd` 2026-08-11. `16eb0c8` 2026-08-11 names the stall (*"that is why plans stall"*) and introduces the start gate in the same commit. `4d7df10` 2026-08-12 adds the agent-agnostic tier claim. `de5358d` 2026-08-13 is the failed gate-drop. `5cf5c5b` 2026-08-19 adds the announcement contract. The report lands 2026-08-24, five days later. **(d) An ablation pins the announcements to one block and clears the worker section.** Four arms, identical fixture and prompt, all with the skill delivered via `.agents/skills/`, `HOME` redirected, differing only in which section of SKILL.md was deleted (`scratch/dev-skill-delegates-to-a-worker-the-agent-does-not-have/ablate.sh`): **F full — 6 `About to:`; G minus the Announce block (`:29-61`) — 0; H minus the worker section (`:155-164`) — 6; I minus both — 0.** The announcement count tracks the Announce block exactly and is untouched by removing the worker section. All four arms still read the skill (the 🤖 marker survives in every one) and all four still shipped the feature, so the block is separable and deleting it cost nothing in output. Log volume did **not** separate cleanly (F 10 638, G 11 815, H 12 868, I 5 278), so the earlier suggestion that the skill drives verbosity is not supported — drop it. **(e) Reversal: the worker instruction is the clearest defect in the file but the weakest driver of the reported behaviour.** Across the two skill-carrying arms of the delivery experiment, **0 of 23 and 0 of 27 agent turns** mention a worker, delegation or the main session in the agent's own output — the model read the instruction, could not act on it, said nothing about it, and built inline anyway, resolving the `:122` / `:156` contradiction toward the preamble. Fix priority therefore inverts: the **announcement contract and the five surviving approval / ask-first gates come first**, and the worker imperative is a correctness and portability fix rather than the cause. `f-ai-07` is a separate defect and does not explain this one — all five gates are present in both trees. **Line numbers on this row are stale as of 2026-08-25** — SKILL.md grew 970 -> 996 lines; the patch anchors on text, not line numbers, and the ports wrap the same sentences differently. | `grep -n "don't do the work inline" <repo>/.claude/skills/tina4-developer-*/SKILL.md` in each of the four port repos. Then, for the delivery and behaviour differential, `scratch/dev-skill-delegates-to-a-worker-the-agent-does-not-have/prove.sh` — three isolated `codex exec` arms, and only the two carrying the skill emit `About to:` or 🤖 |

The shipped implementation is `async function initDatabase(config)` reading `config?.url`, so a bare string leaves `config?.url` undefined and execution falls through to `config?.type ?? "sqlite"` — **the app silently runs on SQLite instead of the engine the developer named**, which is the failure `.claude`'s "you MUST" sentence exists to prevent. **(3) python** (`:877`): `.agents` teaches `tina4py stage` and `tina4py deploy promote staging production`. The reasoning is about **cross-repo** drift and it is correct that there is none; it overlooks the **two sibling trees inside the same repo**, which are the copies Codex and Cursor actually read. `CANON` is `$REPO_ROOT/.claude/skills` on top of that, so even a gated skill is only ever compared `.claude`-to-`.claude` — the second gap already recorded as `ALL-FW-05`. **`js:clear` is a checked finding, not an omission:** `tina4-js/SKILL.md` is byte-identical across `.claude`, `.agents` and `.cursor` in **all four** port repos (md5 `ef7829e56b7c`, 52 960 bytes, 12 copies, zero differing lines). The two are independent. Nothing filed upstream. **FIXED on local branches 2026-08-25 — not filed.** `.claude` propagated to `.agents` and `.cursor` in all four repos. **The drift is far larger than the 8 / 6 / 4 / 2 differing lines recorded on 2026-08-24** — measured 2026-08-25 it is 62 / 59 / 60 / 63 lines present in `.claude` and missing from the other two, because upstream `8086b22` and `f1424ce` added the nested-plan-tree, project-layout, ask-backend-framework and plan-first-hard-rule sections to `.claude` alone. The two trees fall further behind every release. **Nothing was overwritten blind:** across all four repos only nine lines existed *only* in `.agents`/`.cursor`, and each was read in context first. All nine are the wrong side — `tina4py stage` / `tina4py deploy promote` (python), which `.claude` corrects as external Rust CLI verbs; a flat *"the `tina4` CLI self-updates"* (ruby, nodejs) where `.claude` explains the Rust client is external and lists which commands `tina4ruby` actually owns; and `metrics`, `initDatabase(url)` and `tina4nodejs metrics` (nodejs), all three already recorded here. **One new fact, verified against the php source rather than the skill text:** `.agents`/`.cursor` place `migrations/` under `src/`, and `.claude` places it at the project root with an explicit *NOT src/migrations/*. `Tina4/Migration.php:25` reads *"Located in migrations/ (project root) by default — the one canonical location"*, `Tina4/App.php:687` resolves `$this->basePath . '/migrations'` and `Tina4/Bootstrap/MCP.php:2132` uses `$projectRoot . '/migrations'`. A php developer following the Codex or Cursor copy puts migrations where the framework never looks. **Second new fact, and the worse one: the reference material was never shipped to either tree at all.** `references/` has never been tracked under `.agents` or `.cursor` in any skill in any repo — yet those SKILL.md copies carry 8 to 10 pointers into it, including an image embed at `:327`. | `f-rt-01` | framework | py:? php:? rb:? nd:filed#57 js:n/a dlp:? cli:n/a | py:? php:? rb:? nd:? js:n/a dlp:? cli:n/a | tina4-nodejs **3.13.103** and **3.13.114** (both released) 2026-08-24 | 2026-08-24 | Stop resolving handler arguments from parsed parameter names. Bind **positionally** for the documented two-argument arity, the way `tina4_python/core/server.py:2034` already does (`len(_remaining) >= 2` → request then response, names ignored), keeping the path-parameter-by-name lookup in front of it. At minimum, never let an unrecognised **first** parameter default to the response: that default is what converts a naming mismatch into a silent authorization hole. | **A route handler whose first parameter is not literally named `req` or `request` is handed the RESPONSE object in its place — silently, with no throw, no log, and a 200 still returned.** Inside such a handler `req.user`, `req.session` and `req.cookies` are all `undefined`, which reads as "the framework built an incomplete request" and is not: the request is complete, the handler was never given it. **Mechanism, `packages/core/src/server.ts:1002` (`invokeRouteHandler`).** It parses the handler's parameter names out of the function's own source text — `match.handler.toString()`, then `/^(?:async\s*)?(?:function\s*\w*)?\s*\(([^)]*)\)/`, then split on `,` — and maps each name: a path parameter wins first, then `name === "request" \|\| name === "req"` yields the request, and **everything else falls through to `return res`**. That final line is a default, not a fallback: there is no positional rule and no arity check, so a two-parameter handler can receive the response in both positions. **Three ordinary handlers land there, all reproduced against the released bytes:** `(ctx, res)` → `arg0=RESPONSE`; `(e, t)` (any build step that mangles parameter names — the names are read from `toString()`, so a minified or bundled handler has none to recover) → `arg0=RESPONSE`; `({ req, res })` destructured → the capture is `{ req, res }`, splitting on `,` yields `{ req` and `res }`, neither matches, and **both** resolve to the response so the handler's own `req` and `res` are `undefined`. The documented `(req, res)` / `(request, response)` spellings pass, which is why this survives every example in the docs and every scaffolded route. **Confirmed present on both 3.13.103 — the version in the upstream report — and 3.13.114, the current release.** Proof project: [`scratch/route-handler-receives-the-response-as-its-request`](../scratch/route-handler-receives-the-response-as-its-request/), which extracts the shipped `invokeRouteHandler` out of `packages/core/dist/index.js` and calls it, so the reproduction runs released bytes with no server and no patched workspace. **Upstream #57 states the symptom correctly and the mechanism wrongly** (*"Tina4 then supplies an incomplete request object"*); it was opened by an agent that added its request-shape diagnostic **after** filing. Correct the mechanism there rather than opening a second issue. **Cross-port: read, not run — which is why three ports read `?`.** `tina4-ruby/lib/tina4/dispatch_pipeline.rb:692` is name-keyed on `:request`/`:req` with `else resp`, the same shape, and looks affected. `tina4-php/Tina4/Router.php:1562` resolves by **type hint** (`Request::class`) as well as by name and adds a two-parameter positional fallback that corrects an unclaimed first argument, so it looks immune to this case. `tina4_python/core/server.py:2034` is positional for two or more remaining parameters and ignores names entirely, so it looks immune. None of those three was executed; do not promote them to `clear` or `affected` without running the reproduction. **Record:** was `ND-FW-01`; scope — nodejs; filing — [#57](https://github.com/tina4stack/tina4-nodejs/issues/57) (filed by a third party 2026-08-24; symptom right, mechanism wrong). | `scratch/route-handler-receives-the-response-as-its-request/prove.sh` — prints one line per handler signature; every `FAIL` row is a handler that received the response where the request belonged |

That tree is **already** incomplete today — it omits `tests/signals.test.ts`, which the scaffold writes — and this fix adds two more files it does not list, `src/vite-env.d.ts` and `tsconfig.node.json`. Both pages will be `stale` once this merges, and the tsconfig excerpt at line 141 is where the `"types": []` explanation should go, since that is the line a developer would otherwise delete. Neither page is edited by this branch: they live in two other repositories and are their own change. Sibling `d-js-01`, a separate mismatch the same read turned up. **Third attack pass 2026-09-03, one new property added.** Every TypeScript file the scaffold writes must be included by exactly one of the two projects: a split is also how a file ends up in neither, and a file no project includes is one `npm run typecheck` stays green over however wrong it is. Measured on a live scaffold with `tsc --listFiles`: 8 files written, 7 in the browser project, 1 (`vite.config.ts`) in the node project, union complete and intersection empty. (1) **The fallback candidate names cannot match any release published since v3.2.0, so every one of them 404s.** `get_binary_name_candidates()` (`src/main.rs:2045-2069`) crosses `{darwin, macos}` with `{arm64, aarch64}` on macOS, yielding `tina4-darwin-arm64`, `tina4-darwin-aarch64`, `tina4-macos-arm64`, `tina4-macos-aarch64`; on Linux amd64 it yields `tina4-linux-amd64` and `tina4-linux-x86_64`. Only the first of each set is published today (`release.yml:80,84`), verified against the asset lists of v3.8.69, v3.8.75, v3.8.77 and v3.8.78 and by direct HTTP: `tina4-linux-amd64`, `tina4-darwin-arm64`, `tina4-darwin-amd64` and `tina4-windows-amd64.exe` return 200 on v3.8.78 and every other generated name returns 404. **They are not invented, and an earlier version of this row was wrong to say so.** `tina4-macos-arm64`, `tina4-macos-x86_64`, `tina4-linux-x86_64` and `tina4-windows-x86_64.exe` were the **real** asset names in v3.1.4 and v3.1.9; the scheme changed at v3.2.0 and has been stable since. `1333e05` (Andre, 2026-03-24, *Fix tina4 update 404, watcher false positives, binary name matching*) added the fallback loop deliberately, documenting it as covering “variations across releases (amd64 vs x86_64, darwin vs macos)”. Only `tina4-darwin-aarch64` and `tina4-macos-aarch64` have never existed in any release. The defect is therefore not that the names are fictional but that the fallback's benefit — reachable only if a *future* release reverted to a pre-v3.2.0 name — has been zero since v3.2.0, while its cost is paid on every local failure. (2) **`download_file()` throws away the reason.** It returns `matches!(status, Ok(s) if s.success())` (`src/main.rs:2095`), so a 404 and a failed local write are the same value, and the loop tries the next candidate either way. (3) **The summary line lists every candidate as if each were a plausible platform**, which is what converts a full disk into "there is no build for your Mac". (4) **A failed update exits 0** — `handle_update()` `return`s on every failure path, so no script or CI step can detect it. **Reported 2026-09-03** by a colleague on macOS arm64 running 3.8.69, whose first line was `curl: (56) Failure writing output to destination, passed 16375 returned 4294967295` followed by three 404s; he and a second colleague read it as the macOS build being missing. **It is not missing.** Fetched `tina4-darwin-arm64` from v3.8.78 with the same `curl -fsSL -o` the CLI uses: exit 0, 10361248 bytes, `Mach-O 64-bit arm64 executable`, and sha256 `b7f401ffa77d335b5ef496c12f4dca4240462d354e9bf255b250dd574fe8a0e5` matching the published `SHA256SUMS` line for that file exactly. **The message is a local write failure, not a network one.** Reproduced the class by pointing curl at `/dev/full`: `curl: (23) Failure writing output to destination, passed 1378 returned 495` — same wording, same tiny byte count. Exit 23 versus his 56 is curl's HTTP/2 rendering of an aborted transfer, not a different cause. **Reproduced end to end 2026-09-03** with the released **3.8.69** `tina4-linux-amd64` in a `chmod 555` directory, `HOME` and `PATH` redirected: `▶ Trying tina4-linux-amd64 ...` / `curl: (23) client returned ERROR on write of 16375 bytes` / `▶ Trying tina4-linux-x86_64 ...` / `curl: (22) The requested URL returned error: 404` / `✗ Download failed (tried: tina4-linux-amd64, tina4-linux-x86_64)`. **16375 is the same byte count the macOS report shows**, which is curl's buffer, and the process exit code was **0**. **Cross-port check:** the update path is Rust CLI only; no framework port has one, so every other column is `n/a`. **The reporter's own root cause was never confirmed — no word back, as of 2026-09-03.** Whether his disk was full, the directory was not writable, or something else, is unknown and unknowable from here. That does not weaken this row: the defect recorded here is the **misreporting**, which was reproduced independently of his machine and is what `0265491` fixes. ## Provenance

| Source | Rows | What stayed behind |
|---|---|---|
| `findings-log.md` → Known Issues Log (removed 2026-08-19) | 67 | Coverage → `documentation-testing/coverage-ledger/README.md`; `FIX-NN` → [`suggested-fixes.md`](suggested-fixes.md); audit history → `documentation-testing/audit-log.md` |
| `agent-testing/codex-skill-delivery/README.md` → `CODX-01`..`07` | 7 | The mechanism study and the seven-tool delivery scorecard |
| Seeded from backlog G1 / G1a / G4 / G10 / G11 | 10 | The backlog rows, which track the *work* rather than the issue |

The migration was scripted, not transcribed, and asserted three things before writing: that all 67
rows parsed, that every note is byte-recoverable from its source line, and that all 39 upstream
links survived. Three rows (`PY-12-01`, `PY-12-07`, `BH-51`) carry raw `|` characters inside their
notes — from a `datetime | None` signature and pytest parameter lists — which a naive table parse
truncates silently. They are whole here.

**Deliberately not migrated:** the since-removed agent-testing leads file. Those five entries are
*unverified leads*, not confirmed issues — two are already duplicates of rows in this table, and the
rest have no quoted documented claim behind them. They stay a triage queue, and a lead earns a row
here only once it has been reproduced.

**`Kind` was derived, not asserted.** Migrated rows had no such column, so it was classified from
each note: a note quoting an exception, a crash, or behaviour that silently does nothing reads
`framework`; a note about what a page says reads `documentation`. Where both appeared the row reads
`framework`, since behaviour outranks prose. Spot-check it when you next touch a row.

## Rules for a row

- **Reproduce before you write it.** How-to-reproduce is the command or the sequence, not prose. A
  row nobody can reproduce is a rumour.
- **Version-stamp what you actually observed**, and say which part is fresh. If only the source was
  re-read, say so in the `Version` cell rather than implying a run.
- **Record, never fix.** The framework is read-only in this repo —
  `documentation-testing/readme.md` rules 10 to 12.
- **Never document a fault on a page.** Defects are fixed in the framework; documentation states
  intended behaviour. That rule is why several of these rows exist instead of paragraphs on
  tina4.com.
- **A `PY-NN-NN` row still needs a chapter trace.** Rules 11 and 12 are unchanged by the move: that
  ID form is earned only by a quoted documented claim tested inside `documentation-testing/`. Rows
  from the other evaluation axes take a scope-kind code instead.
